Task 5 tests outside the plan's named list: `test_scene.gd` S3 gains one physics call before its "HUD rejection reason" check; OV11 (`test_order_voice.gd`) feeds its older rejection through `_sound.collect_step` instead of `stepped_results`, since the beep it checked came from `_submit` re-reading the note. Assertions unchanged.

Task 6 test measure: `navigation_builds()` counts only previews that need a full grid (`construction.gd:91`, skipped when `blocks_locally`), so it stays 0 on the Bs6 fixture world. RP3 (Bs6 rewritten as `test_sb2_preview_cost`) counts answers by identity of `preview_answer()` instead (unchanged object = no answer; it cannot see a discarded synchronous `preview_build`, ruled minor). RP6 and RP8 assert the answer's content and colour. RP5's online half lives in `test_online_scene.gd` as `test_sb2_rematch_drops_pending_preview`; RP8 builds its catch-up credit after tick 8: at tick 4 the faction commands have not yet created the builders (they appear in step 4 to 5), so `_place` cannot enter placement; a fixture timing, not a bug. After review RP8 also has the peer place a building on the spot in the frame's second tick, so the answer must be refused (computed after the last tick); moving `_answer_preview` into the catch-up loop fails it (mutation run, restored). `_enter_placement` also sends `request_preview({})`, so re-entering the same kind without Esc never stores an old answer.

#### Steam Deck (Task 8 Step 3)

Report-only (answers 10 and 11), host "Steam Deck, Linux native" (Godot 4.7.2 Linux build checked against the CI SHA-256, headless, one `systemd-run --user` unit, XDG folders redirected into one folder under `~`). 2026-11-16. Trees: base `git archive` of `bad67c3`, branch `git archive` of `817abff`. ACAD 1 at every job edge; Deck 1-min load 0.12 at the first start, 0.72-0.78 at later starts (the previous run's own tail), 1.0 at run ends (the benchmark's single thread). No Mac benchmark ran.

Command (scratch script `sb3_team.gd`, outside the repo, copied into each Deck tree): the team-match workload of `run_profile.gd` `_team_match` (`TeamMatch.step`, then `set_view(sim.view_state(), [])` per tick, Profiler on), capped at tick 15000 (not the full 38786-tick match; about 150 s per run), 300 warm-up ticks dropped, 14700 samples. Interleaved base, branch, base, branch, base, branch (20 s gaps); all six exit 0, `peak_sampled_entities=265`. `step` is the `Simulation.step` zone only (bots excluded).

| Row (ms per tick) | base median of 3 [range] | branch median of 3 [range] | branch / base |
|---|---|---|---|
| step mean | 4.9387 [4.9343-4.9489] | 4.9853 [4.9795-4.9996] | 1.009 |
| step p95 | 8.643 [8.633-8.666] | 8.735 [8.706-8.757] | 1.011 |
| step p99 | 10.908 [10.887-10.995] | 11.059 [11.005-11.074] | 1.014 |
| view_state mean | 1.1845 [1.1824-1.1875] | 1.1855 [1.1833-1.1875] | 1.001 |
| view_state p95 / p99 | 1.731 / 1.812 | 1.724 / 1.813 | 1.0 |

Per run (step mean / p95 / p99; view_state mean): base 1: 4.9387 / 8.643 / 10.887; 1.1824. Branch 1: 4.9795 / 8.706 / 11.005; 1.1855. Base 2: 4.9489 / 8.666 / 10.995; 1.1875. Branch 2: 4.9996 / 8.757 / 11.074; 1.1875. Base 3: 4.9343 / 8.633 / 10.908; 1.1845. Branch 3: 4.9853 / 8.735 / 11.059; 1.1833.

Answer 10, view build split (branch only; the zones do not exist on base): `view.rows` mean 0.9084 [0.9081-0.9103] ms (p95 1.419, p99 1.486), `view.buildings` mean 0.1259 [0.1256-0.1270] ms (p95 0.159, p99 0.195), of a `view_state` mean of 1.1855 ms: unit rows are 77 %, building copies 11 %, the rest (map snapshot, players, sources, command results, match) about 0.15 ms or 12 %. The whole view build is 24 % of the step mean at this size. The view build is unchanged by SB3 (1.1845 base, 1.1855 branch).

Answer 11, fresh `view_state()` against filling a kept slot dictionary (scratch script `fill_vs_fresh.gd`, outside the repo; the fill writes the same eight expressions of `view_state` into one `Dictionary` after `clear()`; the check `same_keys_and_rows=true` compares every value, and the unit columns, to a fresh call). Team state at tick 8000 (258 entities), 200 calls per timing, 3 runs of 2 passes each: fresh 1.3322 ms median [1.3146-1.4758], kept slot 1.3536 ms [1.3309-1.5048], kept / fresh 1.016. Per-run medians fresh / kept: 1.339 / 1.357, 1.330 / 1.341, 1.324 / 1.344. Reading: filling a kept dictionary saves nothing measurable; the cost is the data (rows, copies, snapshots), not the 8-key dictionary. A first fill run (same numbers within 1 %) had a faulty equality check (String against StringName keys) and was rerun with the fixed check.

Reading of the step rows: the branch is +0.9 % on step mean and +1-1.4 % on p95 and p99, with ranges that do not overlap (3 runs each, interleaved). The step zone does contain SB3 code (corrected 2026-11-18): the reach cache moved to `Registry.reach_cache`, read twice per `Combat._reach` (`combat.gd:84-85`), and `step()` gained a ring check (`simulation.gd:669`); together an estimated 0.4-0.5 % of the step, against the +0.9 % found (the two new zones inside `view_state` sit outside it); the rest is not identified. Report-only.

Task 3 note: after review the ring-off skip is observable by turning the ring on after ten off ticks (the ten rows read zero), so the always-write mutation is caught. An insert into a frozen cache cannot be tested directly: the engine prints `ERROR:` / `SCRIPT ERROR`, which fails the whole suite by design (exit 1), so the late-insert guard is the warm-covers checks plus that suite-level failure.

Task 4 notes: the plan's "nothing was sent" example `test_skirmish_scene.gd:150` (K4 terminal match) gets no `queued().is_empty()` clause, because that order legitimately waits in the queue and its refusal is checked one tick later (`Rejected: Match ended`). Bs4 (`test_base_scene.gd:192` at base) does not use the `_pending` helper (that helper lives in `test_skirmish_scene.gd`), so it reads `queued()` directly. `_drain_notes` appends a note's results to `stepped_results` only when the note's tick advanced, as the old `if advanced` did; a refused order on a finished match otherwise re-adds the last tick's results once.

Task 5 notes: the plan estimated "about 58 of the 71" `_refresh()` sites for `publish`; 19 need it. The estimate counted `driver.sim = X` sites, but that assignment adopts and job 0 fills CURRENT, so a change made before the assignment is already in the slot. Sites that change the live sim and then send input with no refresh (H3 `remove_object` then right-click, RB4 before its input, AN20 credits, PO gather submits) keep their order: their input reads the shown view by design (SB1), and none has a refresh to replace. The static check's `test_new_files_clean` was red on b8d40ee (Task 4 review test read `main._visuals._pending`); `test_sb3_online_two_notes` now records visual ticks through a `VisualSpy` subclass in the test file, same assertion, so no production change and no allowlist entry. `online_driver.gd` keeps the `sim` bridge property for tests; it no longer reads it itself (`match_log` reads `current().view.players`). After review, the offline log is the pump's recorder (`shortcut:` until SB5 moves it into the record query), so `test_sb3_match_log_equals_recorder` now holds by construction; the undrained-job checks in `test_sb3_record_reads_live_without_queue` carry the log-equals-digest guarantee.

Benchmark comparison base (ms, `build/sb3/base/bench.log`): movement open p95 0.773 p99 0.846; movement dense p95 0.744 p99 0.897; combat open p95 1.893 p99 2.230; dense p95 1.847 p99 2.148; contact p95 1.179 p99 1.55; skirmish3v3 p95 1.348 p99 1.449; transports p95 1.13 p99 1.228; defences p95 0.784 p99 2.161; docks p95 0.591 p99 0.611; hordes300 p95 2.897 p99 3.381; ramp p95 0.651 p99 1.211; frc p95 0.889 p99 1.084; lockstep overhead p99 0.072 (limit 0.6); team_match profile `step` mean 1.355 p95 2.144, `view_state` mean 0.3022 (22.3 % of step, p95 0.403). Budget 1.82/3.64: open, dense and hordes300 exceed p95, as accepted before.

Base traces: `build/sb3/base/{replay,economy,combat,team}.tsv`, `build/sb3/base/netplay/s1.tsv` (untracked). "Traces equal" later: same commands into `build/sb3/<task>/`, then `python3 tools/compare_replays.py` base against branch (replay, economy, combat; team `--interval 200 --rows 100`; `netplay/s1.tsv` `--interval 30 --rows 0`) → `REPLAYS MATCHED`.

The 13 **multi** methods are the allowed-edit-2 list beside the ten Profiler cases: `test_base_scene.gd` `test_f33_red_ghost_refused_after_a_two_step_frame`, `test_preview_once_per_physics_step`; `test_online_scene.gd` `test_sb3_online_two_notes`, `test_sb4_three_callbacks_one_drain`, `test_sb4_no_send_after_end`, `test_sb4_foreign_note_ends_match`, `test_ph_receive_ends_lockstep_with_undrained_job`, `test_ph_preview_on_last_job_of_catch_up`, `test_f33_online_red_ghost_refused_after_two_callbacks`; `test_skirmish_scene.gd` `test_offline_driver_no_tick_hash_rows_after_the_end`, `test_sb3_restart_drops_old_notes`, `test_sb3_process_drains`, `test_sb3_record_reads_live_without_queue`. A callback that takes two ticks (catch-up, `CATCH_UP_STEPS` 2) and is drained through `BaseScene.tick` is not **multi**: the idle wait covers both jobs, so it is **settle**; the cases that rely on it are `test_online_scene.gd` `test_create_resets_catch_up_credit`, `test_catch_up_is_bounded`, `test_two_tick_frame_keeps_earlier_results`, `test_sb2_preview_after_catch_up`, `test_sb4_take_ahead_past_end` and `test_sb4_leave_on_end_tick_catch_up` (both through `_end_in_catch_up`), `test_sb4_no_waiting_after_end`. They are named so Task 9 can move any that goes red into the inline list (a static list cannot see every callback that takes two ticks; `WSUITE` is the check). Task 9 fix round: `test_sb3_online_two_notes` and `test_ph_preview_on_last_job_of_catch_up` are one callback that takes two ticks, so by this rule they are settle, not multi: each got one `BaseScene.settle(main)` after its physics call and left the inline list (21 entries: 11 batching, 10 Profiler). Settle sites beyond this section's lists: `test_online_scene.gd` `test_restart_keeps_map_and_starts` (free-running, read through `live()`); `test_graphics_settings.gd:146` and `test_online_scene.gd:2210` are listed settle but need no edit; the launcher reads (`test_launcher.gd:1476`, `:1696`, `test_online_scene.gd:903`, `:938`, `:964`, `:1047`, `:1146`) have no await between start and read, so no job is in flight.

Free-running scenes (physics runs by itself during `await`; no helper steps them): `test_scene.gd` `test_driver` :182-189 (asserts `tick > before` after one physics frame: **settle** needed before the assertion); the scenes a launcher starts and then reads, which read `pump.live()` while a worker may run, so they need a settle before the read: `test_launcher.gd` `_start_through_wire` :1476, `test_guest_start_matches_host` :1696; `test_online_scene.gd` `test_launcher_faction_rows` :903, `test_plays_dry_wells_from_each_seat` :938, `test_online_start_saves_picks` :964, `test_joiner_refuses_other_map` :1043 and :1047, `test_both_seats_share_map_and_starts` :1146; `test_view_caps.gd` `_match` :37 (physics off at :39). Other suites instantiate scenes without turning physics off (`test_graphics_settings.gd` 7, `test_audio_settings.gd` 4, `test_launcher.gd` 20, `test_playtest.gd` 3, `test_join_link.gd` 2, `test_playtest_overlay.gd` `_open_launcher` :593): none reads a tick or `live()` (checked with `rg`), their pumps are stopped by `_exit_tree` (Task 5) or the runner. `test_benchmark.gd` `Probe` cases have no pump.

#### Static variables in the simulation (9) and the statics `run_job` reaches outside it

Classification as in Ownership "Two pumps at once". "Miss fill" is a write that runs only when a key is missing; `start()` warms every registered kind first (`_warm_and_freeze`, `sim_pump.gd:145-152`: `Registry.freeze_caches()`, then `Skirmish.freeze_map()`), and `test_sb3_freeze_covers_match` (`test_sim_pump.gd:409`) pins that no cache grows over a 900-tick bot match, a 150-tick battle of every armed kind and 300 team-match ticks.

| Static | Declared | Class | Write sites | Mark |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `_extra` | `definitions.gd:15` | test registry (not in `_named_caches`) | `:57` `add_test_kind`, `:61` `clear_test_kinds` | before the freeze (tests only; test resets never run while a worker is active) |
| `_unit_cache` | `:16` | named cache, recursively frozen | `:69` miss fill, `:152` `_reset` | before the freeze; the fill is dead after warm-up |
| `_building_cache` | `:17` | named cache | `:76` miss fill, `:153` `_reset` | before the freeze; dead after warm-up |
| `_profile_cache` | `:18` | named cache | `:83` miss fill, `:154` `_reset` | before the freeze; dead after warm-up |
| `radius_cache` | `:19` | named cache | `combat_definitions.gd:25` miss fill, `definitions.gd:155` `_reset` | before the freeze; dead after warm-up |
| `reach_cache` | `:20` | named cache | `combat.gd:84` miss fill, `definitions.gd:156` `_reset` | before the freeze; dead after warm-up |
| `air_kinds` | `:21` | derived lookup, not in `_named_caches` | `:157` `_reset` only (`_air_kinds()`) | before the freeze; read-only from `run_job` |
| `_skirmish_map` | `skirmish.gd:33` | outer dictionary frozen | `:75` miss fill in `_default_map()`; frozen by `freeze_map` `:68-69` | before the freeze (`start()` fills it; `_slot` `:80` and `:615` only read it) |
| `last_cells_examined` | `destination_allocation.gd:3` | shared diagnostic | `:23` reset, `:72` increment (inside allocation, reachable from `run_job` after the freeze) | reachable after the freeze: the allowed allocation diagnostic, read by `test_allocation.gd:54` |

Statics outside `game/scripts/simulation` that `run_job` reaches: `debug/profiler.gd:6-11` (`enabled`, `slow`, `_current`, `_calls`, `_series`, `_ticks`), written by `end` (`:16-22`) and `end_tick` (`:23-34`) only while `enabled`, and by `reset` (`:35-40`), which tests and `movement_benchmark.gd:261-262,287` call. They are reachable from `run_job` after the freeze but off in production; worker mode asserts `not Profiler.enabled` at `start()` (Ownership, Profiler) and the ten Profiler cases run inline. `playtest/command_recorder.gd` has no static (fields `_sim`, `_commands`), and neither does `PlanRecorder` (`sim_pump.gd:25-29`, P7). `debug/timing_stats.gd` is reached only by `Profiler.report`. No static write was found that is reachable from `run_job` after warm-up other than the allocation diagnostic and the Profiler (off), so no stop.

#### Writes into frozen cache members (Decided 11)

`rg -n '\]\.[a-z_]+\[[^]]+\] *[-+*/]?=' game/scripts/simulation` has no hit (exit 1), so no `cache[key].field[i] = v` writer exists and the Decided 11 stop does not fire. Broader reading of every `Registry.<cache>[...] =` and `_<cache>[...] =` write (`rg -n "_cache\[|_extra\[" game/scripts/simulation`): only the top-level miss fills in the table above (`definitions.gd:57,69,76,83`, `combat_definitions.gd:25`, `combat.gd:84`); each is "not a cache member write" (it inserts a new key into the named cache) and dead after warm-up. They are the places where a kind missing from the warm-up would write into a frozen dictionary: Task 3's freeze change and the Task 3 Step 0 probe cover them.

#### Pump boundary check and the catch-up rule

`PUMP_COUNTS` (`tools/test_sim_boundary.py:101-105`): `offline_driver.gd` 10 and `online_driver.gd` 9, recounted with the check's own regex over the base files (10 and 9; P7 added none); `game/scripts/debug/` is excluded at `:95` (`SCAN_EXCLUDED`). Catch-up rule: `CATCH_UP_STEPS = 2` (`online_driver.gd:12`), `MAX_CATCH_UP_CREDIT = 4` (`:13`), credit gained at `:127`, loop at `:128`; the loop stops at `_last.over or _stopped` (`:129`).

#### Engine calls reachable from `run_job`

Scan: `rg -n "FileAccess|ResourceLoader|DisplayServer|OS\.|Time\.|print\(|printerr|push_warning|push_error|JavaScriptBridge|Engine\."` over `game/scripts/simulation`, `game/scripts/match/sim_pump.gd`, `game/scripts/playtest/command_recorder.gd` and `game/scripts/debug/profiler.gd`; the call graph from `run_job` leaves `game/scripts/simulation` only into `debug/profiler.gd` (preloaded by `state_hash.gd`, `skirmish.gd`, `simulation.gd`, `grid_navigation.gd`, `combat.gd`), `debug/timing_stats.gd` and `playtest/command_recorder.gd`.

| Call | Site | Mark |
|---|---|---|
| `FileAccess.open` | `simulation/map_data.gd:8-13` (`MapData.load`) | needs the event loop on Web; reached from `run_job` only through `Skirmish._default_map()` (`skirmish.gd:74-76`) behind an empty-cache check that `start()` has already satisfied (`sim_pump.gd:145-152`); `Skirmish.create` (`:91`) loads on main |
| `push_error` | `map_data.gd:10,13`, `skirmish.gd:94` | worker-side console output only on a bad map or starts, both on main in `create`; not reached from `run_job` |
| `Time.get_ticks_usec` | `debug/profiler.gd:14,18` | serviced during the main thread's spin (a clock read, no event loop), and only while `Profiler.enabled` |
| `OS.*`, `DisplayServer`, `ResourceLoader`, `print`, `printerr`, `push_warning`, `JavaScriptBridge` | none in the scanned files | none: an `OS.*` call reachable from `run_job` would be a stop, and there is none |
| `Engine.print_error_messages` | `test_relay_transport.gd:85,89,98,100,106,108` (three off/on pairs; no production site, no site in `.github`, `tools` or `game/scripts`) | the logger's blind spot (Failure channel), tests only |

### Findings for later tasks (Task 1)

1. Pump API at the base is the inline pump only (`sim_pump.gd` 193 lines): no `has_room`, `mode`, `queue_stats`, `failure()` on the drivers beyond `_read_failure()`; Task 2 onward adds them, so the plan's names have no collision.
2. Two existing top-level writes into the named caches (`combat_definitions.gd:25` radius, `combat.gd:84` reach) are miss fills, not member writes; see "Writes into frozen cache members". Task 3's `_freeze` change and probe should state them in the ledger.
3. `drain` in `online_driver.gd` already carries P7's `_pings.append_array(_new_pings(notes))` (`:157`); Task 7's `drain` hunk (failure read and end) goes around it.
4. The offline `step` already reads the failure on a refused post (`_read_failure()`), as Failure channel "Callers" expects.
5. Task 12's harness patch calls `TeamMatch.create()` and `TeamMatch.step(sim)`; P7 gave both an `entries` parameter with the old `SEATS` default (`debug/team_match.gd:16,26`, plus `SEATS_2V2`), so the calls still compile and mean the 3v3 load.
6. The plan's example `test_two_tick_frame_keeps_earlier_results` is not a multi-callback case (it drives `tick` and a catch-up of two ticks per callback); it is marked settle, with the other catch-up cases, not inline.

### Task 2: the switch, the mode, and where it shows

Built on the Task 1 commit; the pump still runs every job inline in both modes. Tree for every number: the working tree on top of `323246b3`.

- `sim_pump.gd`: `static worker_wanted()` (`WARGRID_SIM_THREAD == "1"`, threads feature, latch not set), `static force_mode_for_test(mode)`, `static reset_process_latch_for_test()`, `mode()` fixed in `_init`. The latch is set by `_fail` (the first-failure setter that exists on the base), so a failing pump makes the next pump inline for the process. A forced mode wins over the latch.
- Decided for you (reversible): `mode()` returns `"inline-fallback"` for a pump created with the switch on and the latch set; the plan has the drivers compute that word from `pump.mode()`, but the drivers cannot see the private latch without a further public pump member, and the Contract list allows none. `sim_mode()` is then `pump.mode()` in both drivers, and tasks that branch on the worker use `mode() == "worker"`. A forced-inline test pump says `"inline"`, as does a build without threads.
- `offline_driver.gd`, `online_driver.gd`: `sim_mode()`; online `_report` adds `report["sim"]` (the bracket spelling, because `tools/test_sim_boundary.py` flags `.sim` as a live-simulation read). `PUMP_COUNTS` 10 to 11 (offline) and 9 to 10 (online): one `pump.mode()` each.
- `match_screen.gd`: `_header` has `"sim": driver.sim_mode()` (playtest header and record); `_perf.mode = driver.sim_mode()` beside `_perf.extra` and after `_create_world()` in `_restart_world`.
- `perf_overlay.gd` (text only, agent map Performance entry read: no drawing or budget affected): `var mode`, first line `FPS n | Sim: worker|inline|inline (fallback)`; empty `mode` leaves the line as before; `extra` unchanged below.
- New cases (allowed edit 4): `test_sb5a_mode_from_switch` (`test_sim_pump.gd`), `test_sb5a_f3_shows_mode` (`test_perf_overlay.gd`), `test_sb5a_header_names_mode` (`test_skirmish_scene.gd`), `test_sb5a_online_header_and_report_name_mode` (`test_online_scene.gd`); test statics `latch_for_test()` and `mode_after_latch()` in `test_sim_pump.gd`. Red before the code (12 failed checks, no load errors), green after. The cases restore the environment variable, the latch and the forced mode.
- Review fixes (one round): the F3 word after a restart is pinned (a stale overlay mode must read the new word after `_restart()`; red with the `_restart_world` line removed, restored), plus an overlay unit check for `inline-fallback`; the mode checks build unstarted pumps and `latch_for_test()` forces inline for its own pump and stops it, so Task 3's worker start never meets a Task 2 helper; `_init` takes its mode from `static _mode_for()`.
- Constraints for Tasks 3 to 7: `_fail` writes the static `_latched` and must stay on the main thread (the worker queues a marker, main converts it; a worker-side `_fail` is a shared-static write, a stop condition); test code compares `mode() == "worker"`, never `== "inline"`; `mode()` has three values (`worker`, `inline`, `inline-fallback`), and Task 15 states them in `sim-interface.md` and restates Decided 5 as "the pump fixes it in `_init`; `sim_mode()` passes it through".

### Task 3: tests and red (subagent A: Steps 0-2)

Tree for every number: the working tree on top of `36a29706`; logs in `L` by tag.

### Task 3: the queue (subagent B: Steps 3-6)

Tree for every number: the working tree on top of `36a29706` (`sim_pump.gd` staged by `git add` for the mutation proofs); logs in `L` by tag (`t3*`).

- **Implemented in `sim_pump.gd`:** worker mode in `start()` (job zero on the caller, `_spawn()`: threads and Profiler assert, abandoned pumps reaped, `Thread.start`, bounded start handshake recording `_owner_id`); `post` to `_post_queued` (marker read first, refuse at the cap, append and count under the lock, work semaphore); worker loop `_run_worker` / `_run_next` / `_publish` (null note queues the marker `"post"`, drops the waiting jobs and exits; the gate waits `before_publish` and `inside_publish` are bounded `try_wait` loops of `2 * WAIT_MSEC`); `drain` (notes, slot swap, marker under the lock; main converts the marker with `_fail`, then `_watch`: stall clock, `dead thread`, stall by frames and wall time); `_abandon`; `stop()` (quit and drop waiting jobs under the lock, poll the exit up to `WAIT_MSEC`, join, else abandon; idempotent); `busy`, `has_room`, `queue_stats`, `reset_queue_stats`, `cap_for_test`, `gate_publish_for_test`, `live_workers`, `stop_live_for_test`. `live_workers()` is `_threads.size()`: pumps whose thread is started and not joined, abandoned ones included (A's reading, confirmed against the plan's "Thread lifecycle"). `pump_counts()` stays bare (Task 9). Not built here: the owner-thread check `_run_owned`, the `PumpLogger` fault flag (Task 6), query through the worker (Task 4), the dropped-job and restart paths' driver hunks (Task 5), per-job timings in `queue_stats` (Task 12), debug invariants and the `live()` assert.

The marker is a bare cause string (`"post"`), not a dictionary with a tick; the failure text takes the tick from `_current`. `main_wait_*` in `queue_stats` count the start handshake and `stop()` waits. Inline `has_room()` is true unless `cap_for_test` set a cap, then it counts undrained notes. The abandon and dead-thread paths are written but unproven until Task 6's fault processes (no Task 3 case reaches them).

### Task 4: `query` and `record` through the worker

Tree for every number: the working tree on top of `fdf5a647`; logs in `L` by tag (`t4*`).

- **Implemented in `sim_pump.gd`:** `class Query extends RefCounted` (job, note, its own reply semaphore; the queue is now `Array[RefCounted]`, so `Job` stays pure data); worker `query` (`_query_worker`): reads the marker under the lock first (a pending marker fails the pump, no entry), else appends the entry behind the waiting tick jobs, posts the work semaphore and polls `try_wait` up to `WAIT_MSEC`; expiry is `_abandon("stall: query waited 5000 ms")` and null; a null reply (the queried job returned no note, or a failed job dropped it) reads the marker (`"query"` or the earlier `"post"`) and fails the pump. The worker answers the entry on its own thread (`_answer`): no `before_publish` gate, no READY, no count, no `_notes` entry, so slots and drain are untouched. `_drop_waiting()` (under the lock) replaces the two `_queue.size()` subtractions in the marker path and `stop()`: it answers any waiting query with null and counts only jobs, so a query behind a failed job never leaves the count off by one. `query` returns null at once once `failure()` is set, in both modes (inline too; `test_ph_null_job_fails_pump` keeps its first reason). Without a thread (inline, or after a join) `query` runs `_note_for` on the caller. `record()`'s fallback reads `_recorder` only when there is no thread or `_worker_gone()` (exit seen or thread dead); otherwise the log is empty (Stalled trace step 2). `query` counts its wait in `main_wait_*` (Decided 12).
- Decided for you (reversible): the null-query marker is the cause string `"query"`, the same shape as Task 3's `"post"`; the deadline cause text is `stall: query waited <WAIT_MSEC> ms`; no dead-thread check inside the query poll (a dead worker with a pending query ends by the deadline, `dead thread` stays the drain's); `_marker` is never cleared, so a later query on a marker-failed pump also returns null through the early return.
- **Tests (red first):** group (b) of `test_sim_pump.gd` runs in both modes through `_each_mode` (`test_sb3_query_leaves_slots`, `test_sb4_ring_query` with `_post_when_room` between its 40 posts, `test_th_pump_tick_hash_on_request`, `test_ph_record_returns_command_log`, `test_pump_bot_lines_on_request`, `test_planned_bot_commands_not_in_pump_record`); `test_ph_null_job_fails_pump` stays inline. New: `test_sb5a_query_never_touches_slots`, `test_sb5a_query_waits_behind_jobs` (gated worker, a releaser thread frees two held jobs after 150 ms; the query returns tick 2 after at least 100 ms), `test_sb5a_record_waits_for_queue` (40 jobs, three held at the gate when `record()` is called; equals the inline record), `test_sb5a_query_after_stop_runs_inline` (runs on the caller's thread id), `test_sb5a_query_null_on_failed_pump` (both modes, null under 50 ms; plus a stalled worker, which sets no marker, so the early return is the only thing that spares the 5 s deadline), `test_sb5a_record_keeps_commands`, `test_sb5a_record_on_stalled_worker_is_empty` (empty log while the worker lives, the kept log after `_exited`). Driver cases: `test_sb5a_offline_log_equals_inline_in_worker_mode` (`test_skirmish_scene.gd`, `OfflineDriver` with a worker pump: `match_log()` and `record()` equal the inline driver's after 200 steps and a `flush` query) and `test_sb5a_online_start_digest_equals_inline_in_worker_mode` (`test_online_scene.gd`: the frames of an online start, startup digest included, equal inline's).
- **Red:** `ONLY(t4red, test_sb5a_)` (`t4red-only-test_sb5a_.log`) CASES 24 CHECKS 124 FAILURES 6: the query and record waits, the record at tick 38 instead of 40, the null-on-failed-pump check in both modes; named checks only, no load error. The two driver cases and `test_sb5a_query_never_touches_slots`, `test_sb5a_query_after_stop_runs_inline` were already green: equality guards on a tree whose worker query still ran on main.
- **Mutations** (`git add`, Edit, named case red, Edit back, `git diff --exit-code` exit 0; `t4-mut1..3.log`): a query note appended to `_notes` -> `SB5A the query's note is not in the drain queue, got [1, 2, 2]` and the drain-after-record check red; `record()`'s job without `want_log` -> `test_sb5a_record_keeps_commands` red in both modes (0 of 3) plus the record and offline-log checks; `query` without the failure early return -> `test_sb5a_query_null_on_failed_pump` red, inline `[inline]` (0 ms) and the stalled worker (5000 ms). Honest limit: in worker mode after a marker the query path still returns null at once (it reads the persistent marker), so the worker half of the plan's "red in both modes" is the stalled-worker case, not the marker case.
- Step 4 commands: `SUITE(t4)` `t4-suite.log` CASES 1706 CHECKS 160206 (Task 3: 1697 and 160151 at the fix round); `TRACES(t4)` four MATCHED lines; `WONLY(t4w, test_sb5a_)` `t4w-only-test_sb5a_.log` CASES 24 CHECKS 126 exit 0; `CHECKS(t4)` `t4-checks.log`. ### Task 5: stop, exit, restart; runner hunk

Tree for every number: the working tree on top of `7eb48c65` (the four production files staged by `git add` for the mutation proofs); logs in `L` by tag (`t5*`).

- **Red first:** a bare `leaked_worker_line` stub in `run_tests.gd` and the null-job-zero guard without `_fail` (Step 0 stub: `start()` fills `_current` and returns an empty note at the simulation's tick), then `ONLY(t5red4, test_sb5a_)` (`t5red4-only-test_sb5a_.log`): CASES 36 CHECKS 204 FAILURES 17, every failure a named `FAIL: SB5A ...` or `R7` check, no `SCRIPT ERROR`, no load error. The stop cases (idle, in flight, idempotent) and the restart cases were already green: Task 3 built `stop()`, and a restart reaches no old note by construction; they are guards. Two earlier red runs were fixture bugs and are not evidence: the replay compared a pump built with factions against a driver world built without, and the online move was written before the faction chose its units (a move before `choose_faction` is refused, so it never changed a digest).
- **Implemented:** `sim_pump.gd` `start()`: a null job zero returns `_failed_start()`: `_current` filled on main (`tick`, `view_state()`), `_fail("start")` (`start job returned no note after tick N`), no `_spawn`, an empty note at the simulation's tick. Both drivers' `create` stop the old pump before `Skirmish.create`; `adopt` keeps its own `stop` (tests adopt into a running driver). Online: `stop()` is `_stopped = true` plus `_stop_pump()` (stop, return if `pump.failure()` is set, else drain the completed notes through the pairing guard and `_lockstep.record` only, `_last` follows; nothing is sent and no bot is submitted); `_finish()` calls `_stop_pump()` first when `_ended()` (desync, relay loss, protocol end, a `left`), before `_write_report`, so the desync ring query runs inline after the join. The pairing guard of `_finish_job` is now `_paired_tick(note) -> int` (-1 for none); `_finish_job` keeps its text from `_lockstep.record` on (P7's hunk). `match_screen._exit_tree` calls `driver.stop()` after `orders._set_ping(false)` and before `driver.detach()` (the plan says after `_set_attack_move(false)`; the ping cleanup that P7 added sits between, and stays). `run_tests.gd`: `SimPump` preload, `LEFT_TO_RUNNER` (Task 1's 13 "runner" marks), `static leaked_worker_line(case, stopped)`, `static switch_on()`, and the per-case hunk after the awaited call (`SimPump.stop_live_for_test()`, `reset_process_latch_for_test()`, `force_mode_for_test("")`, under the switch a leak not on the list prints `FAIL: live sim worker after <case>` and fails the run); the shard ends with `SIM PUMPS LIVE <n>` under the switch (`SimPump.live_workers()`, abandoned pumps counted, so Task 6's processes will not use it). `tools/test_sim_boundary.py`: `PUMP_COUNTS` offline 12 (+1: create), online 13 (+1 create, +2 `_stop_pump` reads `failure()` and drains).

### Task 6: fault flag and the fault processes

Tree for every number: the working tree on top of `ebbc9c9a` (`sim_pump.gd` staged by `git add` for the mutation proofs); logs in `L` by tag (`t6*`, exit statuses `t6-exit-1..10`).

- **Implemented:** `pump_logger.gd` (`PumpLogger extends Logger`: `_log_error` counts per `OS.get_thread_caller_id()` under its own mutex, warnings not counted, no `_log_message`, `count_for(id)`). `sim_pump.gd`: `_run_owned(job, slot)` is the one place a job runs (start, inline post, inline query, worker loop, worker query answer all call it; `_note_for` stays the test seam inside it): a caller that is not `_owner_id` gets `push_error`, the marker `owner thread` and null; a worker's job (`_thread != null` and a logger installed) whose thread's error count rose gets the marker `fault` and null, so the faulted note is never appended (Task 3 L5). `_set_marker` sets the marker only if empty; `_publish` and `_answer` keep an earlier marker and still go through `_drop_waiting()`. `_install_logger()` runs in `_spawn` on main before `Thread.start`, debug builds only, never removed. `_fail` words the two new causes (`fault: a job logged an error at tick N`, `owner thread: a job ran off its thread at tick N`); the latch is already set by `_set_failure`.
- Decided for you (reversible): inline pumps are not fault-watched (an error logged on main inside an inline job stays today's behaviour, so the switch off changes nothing); `mode()` after a failure is `"inline-fallback"` (Task 2), so `next_match_inline` asserts "not worker" and not the plan's word `"inline"`; `fault` watches only jobs on the worker, so job zero, a query after a join and a record after `stop()` are not watched; `pump_faults.gd` has no lambdas (a first lambda-based hook fixture ran once as if it returned at once, `stop() took 0 ms`, not reproduced in 6 direct runs and 3 script runs; the fixture is now a `HookPump` with plain fields). The `run_pump_faults.sh` outer bound defaults to 300 s per case (handoff; the plan said 20 s), `sh tools/run_pump_faults.sh [cases] [bound_seconds]` takes a space-separated case list and a bound for the mutation proofs. `FAULTS(t6)` imports first, sets `WARGRID_SIM_THREAD=1` itself and judges all ten cases by the `FAULT CASE <name> PASSED` marker plus exit 0.
- **Cases (10):** `logged_error`, `main_error_control` (a main-thread `push_error` while the worker is inside a job: no fault), `wrong_thread` (the main thread calls `_run_owned` on a worker pump), `fault_in_query`, `stall_restart` (stall found by `drain` after 90 frames and 3 s, then a restart pump is `inline-fallback` and runs 30 jobs), `stall_leave` (a leave after the stall returns at once; `record()` has no digest and an empty log although a command was recorded earlier; pump level only), `stall_stop` (`stop()` on a stuck worker takes the 5000 ms deadline, `stall: stop waited 5000 ms`, no join; a following `live()` asserts, in debug, on the abandoned pump), `stall_query` (`query()` returns null at the deadline, `stall: query waited 5000 ms`), `dead_thread` (a worker that returns at once, `dead thread`, joined), `next_match_inline`. The abandon, stall and dead-thread paths Task 3 and 4 wrote unproven were green on their first run; the four fault cases and `next_match_inline` were red for the right reason first.
- **Red** (`t6red-faults.log`, Step 0 tree: `_run_owned` a bare passthrough): `logged_error` (`failure is ''`), `wrong_thread` (`a job ran on a thread that is not the owner`), `fault_in_query` (`a faulted query returned a note`), `next_match_inline` (`the fixture did not fail the pump`) red with named reasons; `main_error_control` green as a control, the four stall cases and `dead_thread` green.
- **Mutations** (`git add`, Edit, named case red, Edit back, `git diff --exit-code` exit 0; `t6-mut1..7`): the logger compare skipped (`if false and ...`) -> `logged_error`, `fault_in_query`, `next_match_inline` red; owner check skipped -> `wrong_thread` red; the stop deadline removed -> `stall_stop` `FAILED: still running after 20 s` (outer bound, run as `sh tools/run_pump_faults.sh stall_stop 20`); `_answer` calling `_note_for` -> `fault_in_query` red; the dead-thread check skipped -> `dead_thread` red (the stall check ends it later); the latch write removed from `_set_failure` -> `next_match_inline` and `stall_restart` red; `record()`'s fallback reading `_recorder` beside a living worker -> `stall_leave` red. Fix round: `live()` guarded by `busy()` only (the plan's `stall_stop` mutation) -> `stall_stop` red (`live() on an abandoned pump did not assert`, `t6r2-mut-live.log`); `live()` now asserts `not busy() and not _abandoned` (Ownership), the one `sim_pump.gd` change of the round.
- **Abandoned-thread exit status on macOS, probe record** (Darwin 27.2, Apple silicon, godot 4.7.2 debug; first fixture: a worker held inside a job for 60 s, so still inside it at exit; 10 full runs of the four stall cases, `t6-exit-1..10.log`, not part of the gate): marker `PASSED` in 40 of 40, exit status `stall_restart` 7 x 0 and 3 x 134, `stall_leave` 9 x 0 and 1 x 134, `stall_stop` 9 x 0 and 1 x 134, `stall_query` 8 x 0 and 2 x 134 (an earlier lambda-hook fixture: 13 of 14 aborted). 134 is SIGABRT: `A Thread object is being destroyed without its completion having been realized` (`drivers/apple/thread_apple.cpp:150`), `Internal script error! Opcode: 0` in the worker's GDScript (`t6-flake-2..6.log`), then `libc++abi: recursive_mutex lock failed`; `t6-flake-5.log` is a SIGSEGV. The worker runs script bytecode the engine has already freed: a use-after-free at teardown, not a pump verdict. Linux's "5 of 5 clean" in the critique's probe is not evidence of safety. The plan's Task 6 text asks for exactly this probe; the gate does not contain it. **Fix round (review `t6-review.md`):** the held jobs are finite (`HOLD_MSEC` 12 s, released by the case once its assertions are done), each stall case waits (bounded) for the abandoned worker to exit, reaps it and asserts `live_workers() == 0` before `quit()`, and `run_pump_faults.sh` judges all ten cases by marker plus exit 0 again. Stability after the fix (`t6r2-stall-1..12.log`): `stall_restart` 12 x exit 0, `stall_leave` 12 x 0, `stall_stop` 12 x 0, `stall_query` 12 x 0, marker `PASSED` 48 of 48; the full `FAULTS` run exit 0 each time. The two late-publish paths (a stalled job that ends after its pump was abandoned: the worker publishes into the abandoned pump, sees quit and exits) now run in a real process. For the owner: whether SB5a must make "quit after a stall" safe (a quit path that ends the process without script teardown while an abandoned worker lives, accepting the crash because a stall is already a bug state, or SB5b/SB6), and whether a "thread inside a job at exit" probe case, judged by marker alone, may join the gate or CI for Linux and Windows. Not separated: whether `PumpLogger` (a script logger called during teardown) adds to the abort; a probe with the logger not installed would say. Linux and Windows exit statuses of the gate cases come from the CI `FAULTS` step (Task 9).
- **Step commands:** `FAULTS(t6)` `t6g2-faults-1..3.log` (three full runs, `PUMP FAULTS PASSED 10`, exit 0 each); `SUITE(t6)` `t6-suite.log` CASES 1718 CHECKS 160296 FAILURES 0 (Task 5: the same, no suite case added), exit 0; `TRACES(t6)` `t6-traces.log` four MATCHED lines; `CHECKS(t6)` `t6-checks.log` CHECKS PASSED 5; `t6-lint.log`, `t6-warnings.log`, `t6-python.log`, `t6-shell.log` exit 0; `WONLY(t6w, test_sb5a_)` and `ONLY(t6, test_sim_pump)` as in the resume record. `uptime` 1.66 at the first red run, 1.89 at SUITE, 2.45 at the exit-status loop start (all runs green, no rerun owed).
- **Carry-forwards:** (1) Task 7: pin `OnlineDriver._stop_pump`'s early return for a failed pump (`online_driver.gd:117`) in `stall_online`; the pump-level `stall_leave` does not cover it (Task 5 L4 stays open until then). (2) `tools/test_sim_boundary.py` does not yet fail when a file other than `sim_pump.gd` defines `_run_owned` (the Ownership rule; no task named the hunk; owner task 7 or 15). (3) `stall_online` and `stall_offline` are Task 7's cases. (4) Task 7 decides what `drain` does with notes of an abandoned pump or after a main-side marker (the Failure channel says it drops them; today it returns them, and a main-set `owner thread` marker does not stop the worker; unreachable in production). (5) The failure text counts `_current.tick + 1`, so a fault in a query or with undrained notes names a tick one late (text only). (7) The stale block at the top of this file (Task 4 resume record) was not touched; the `## SB5a` record is the live one.

Test first: `stall_offline` and `stall_online` (`pump_faults.gd`) now release the held job, wait for the worker (`_await_gone`) and call the driver's `drain()`; red without the fix (`t7fix-red.log`: `drain() handed out the late note ... under the freeze card`, `... to the ended match`); then the one-line `if _abandoned: return []` at the top of `SimPump.drain()` (ruled in scope by the coordinator, ledgered, reversible; this supersedes Task 6 carry-forward 4). Green: both cases pass 6 of 6 (`t7fix-stall-1..6.log`, `stall_offline` about 3070 ms, `stall_online` about 4306 ms, exit 0), mutation (line removed with Edit, `t7fix-m1.log`) red, revert `git diff --exit-code` exit 0. L5: `test_sb5a_offline_waits_for_room` gained a `room_refusals == 2` check (the fixture's own `has_room()` plus the step's), so the worker half also goes red without the offline room check (`t7fix-l5-red.log`, 3 checks, inline and worker); a refused worker post alone keeps the queue and cannot tell the two apart. L6: `test_only_the_pump_defines_run_owned` red with a temporary `func _run_owned` in `game/tests/failing_pump.gd` (`['game/scripts/match/sim_pump.gd', 'game/tests/failing_pump.gd'] != ['game/scripts/match/sim_pump.gd']`, `t7fix-l6-red.log`), reverted with Edit, `git diff --exit-code` exit 0. After the round: `ONLY`/`WONLY(test_sim_pump)` CASES 91 CHECKS 611 FAILURES 0; `ONLY`/`WONLY(test_sb5a_)` CASES 56 CHECKS 331 FAILURES 0, `SIM PUMPS LIVE 0`; `FAULTS` `PUMP FAULTS PASSED 12`, all twelve exit 0; `SUITE(t7f)` CASES 1738 CHECKS 160468 FAILURES 0; `TRACES` four `REPLAYS MATCHED`; `NET` match 17.2 s and rematch 30.2 s `NETPLAY PASSED`, `s1.tsv` 325 matched, `s1.cmd` cmp 0; `CHECKS PASSED 5` (load 0.7 to 1.2).
- **Carry-forwards (Task 15 wording):** (1) The failure text names `_current.tick + 1` and can lag by the number of undrained notes when the marker is read in `post` or `query` (not in `drain`); text only. (2) Fifth mutation (`_end_on_failure()` without `_transport.close()`): the plan names `test_sb5a_relay_loss_and_pump_failure_same_frame`, but `_finish()` closes any lockstep end in the same drain, so the explicit close matters on the step path pinned by `test_sb5a_failure_between_room_and_post`. (3) Every pump-failure end closes the transport twice (`_end_on_failure`, then `_finish`); `RelayTransport.close()` is idempotent, so only the peer-visible effect is once. (4) `_failure_ended` (reset in `adopt`, which `net/headless_client.gd:139` calls directly) and `_failure_cleared` (reset in `_restart_world`) sit outside the literal named hunks but are the hook's own state. (5) The online failure end keeps the selection and does not clear a standing preview request or `_awaiting_build`; only the freeze card path resets (plan, Failure channel). (6) The stall cases are the only timing-sensitive ones (3 to 5 s waits).

The loopback is not a production-red test: the production path was already built by Tasks 3 to 7, so it is a new guard, proved by a mutation below.

The line echoes the script's own variable after the `unset`, so it proves the unset ran, not the mode the client chose (the client cannot print its `sim_mode()` without touching `game/scripts/net/`), and nothing fails on a non-empty value: a reader of the log is the check. With the switch exported by the caller, `left-mid-pump` (`t10-np-left-mid-pump.log`, two lines) and `trio-play-on` (`t10-np-trio-play-on.log`, three lines) print an empty value and pass.

Ticks taken per rendered frame after two warm-up frames: inline 10 fps 3, 6 fps 5, 4 fps 5; worker 3 at every fps. The worker 3 is the queue cap `MAX_QUEUED` 3 only while each job outlasts the burst of physics callbacks; publishing frees room before the drain (Capacity), so a faster host or cheaper ticks can reach 4 or 5, and the worker floor below is host-dependent. Ticks per second by fps (`CATCH-UP PACE` lines in `t10-b3.log`, `t10-b4.log`): inline 30:33.0 20:30.0 15:30.0 12:30.0 10:30.0 9:30.6 8:30.4 7:30.8 6:30.0 5:25.0 4:20.0; worker 30:33.0 20:32.0 15:31.5 12:32.4 10:30.0 9:27.0 8:24.0 7:21.0 6:18.0 5:15.0 4:12.0. Lowest fps that keeps 30 ticks/s in this script: inline 6, worker 10 (Capacity's estimate: inline about 6, worker about 10-12). Falling behind below the floor is the accepted limit, not a stop.

### Task 11: digests on four loads, both modes

Tree for every number: the working tree on top of `b3835831` plus Task 11 (uncommitted); logs in `L` by tag (`t11*`). Load 1.2 to 2.4 before the runs; no red needed a rerun.

- **New files:** `game/benchmarks/run_worker_digests.gd` (one process per run, `--layout=<name> --mode=<inline|worker>`; the mode is forced through `force_mode_for_test`, so the switch is not needed; Profiler off), `tools/run_worker_digests.sh`. `run_profile.gd` and every production path are unchanged.
- **Run shape:** skirmish3v3, hordes300 and late_game_3v3 from `CombatWorkload.create` (no bots) and team from `TeamMatch.create()` (the six seats as bots); the end tick per load is 3000, 3000, 3000 and 12000 (the plan leaves it open; about 2.6, 7, 5 and 19 s per run); a post only when `has_room()`, a drain every loop, `want_digest` on every 30th tick (100, 100, 100 and 400 digests per run). A digest job holds the next post until its note is drained, so the shown slot is that tick's slot and `view=` hashes its value projection (every `ViewRows` script variable, read from its property list, plus the other view keys, SHA-256 of `var_to_bytes`); between digests the worker runs free (`depth_max=3` in every worker run, 6436 to 52813 refused posts). Every load gets two human `move` orders (ticks 100 and 400) for the lowest unit, so a divergence has a command to corrupt.
- **Output:** per run `COMMANDS <load> <hash>` (the pump's `record()` log), `RUN <load> mode=... depth_max=... room_refusals=...` and `DIGEST <load> tick=<t> <hash> view=<hash>`. The script compares the command logs first (`COMMAND LOG MISMATCH`: a different input, or bot orders the worker made differently), then the DIGEST lines; it fails on no lines, unequal counts, or a digest or view column that never changes. On a mismatch it reruns the 30-tick window per tick in both modes in one process (`--window=<from>,<to>`) and prints the first differing tick, both runs' commands, digests and view hashes, and the first differing snapshot field (or says that the snapshots are equal); a window that matches says the free-running mismatch depends on timing. Each process is bounded at 300 s without `timeout` (`WORKER_DIGESTS_BOUND`).
- **Self-test:** `sh tools/run_worker_digests.sh --inject-divergence` (first load only, one worker command's target moved by one cell) exits 0 only when both the command-log check and the digest check reported a mismatch, else 1 with `INJECTED DIVERGENCE NOT DETECTED`. What it proves: both comparisons fire on a changed input. The injection is an input, so the digest comparison is proven alone by the production mutations below, not by the self-test. Final tree (`t11-inject-final.log`): `COMMAND LOG MISMATCH`, `DIGEST MISMATCH ... tick 120`, `FIRST DIFFERENCE skirmish3v3 tick=101 ... field snapshot.entities[0].order.target[0] inline 10.5 worker 11.5`, `INJECTED DIVERGENCE DETECTED`, exit 0. An injection that changes nothing (`10.5 if inject else 10.5`) -> exit 1, `command log mismatch 0, digest mismatch 0` (`t11-inject-noop.log`).

### Task 12: match bench and `worker_ab.py`

Tree for every number: the working tree on top of `3597b77d` plus Task 12 (uncommitted); logs in `L` by tag (`t12*`). `uptime` load 1.2 to 1.8 before each bench run (no "host under load" tag); 4.1 after the checks, no run affected. No `run_benchmark.sh` and nothing rendered: the bench ran headless (`Engine.max_fps = 60` there), and the rendered rows are Task 13 and the owner's.

- **Pump (`sim_pump.gd`):** `queue_stats()` gains `jobs`: one row per drained worker job, `tag` (`plain`, or `snapshot+digest` when the job asked for a digest), `job_usec` (`run_job`), `handoff_usec` (end of the step to the end of `run_job`: the note and slot) and `age_usec` (publish to the drain that took the note). The worker stamps a worker-local `_timing` dictionary (`run_job` gets an optional fifth parameter, `_note_for` keeps its two-argument signature for the test subclasses), `_publish` appends it under the lock to `_unread`, `drain` takes the list under the same lock and stamps the drain time outside it; nothing lands on `Note`. Both lists stop at `TIMING_CAP` (4000) rows so a long match cannot grow them; `reset_queue_stats()` also forgets unread rows. Inline pumps produce no rows.
- **Offline driver:** `var bots: Array[int] = BOTS` and `job.bots = bots`; `var digest_every: int = 0` with `job.want_digest = digest_every > 0 and (_next_tick + 1) % digest_every == 0` (behaviour-neutral at 0).
- **Bench (new, from the Deck harness patch, ported by hand):** `bench_loads.gd` (adds `late_game_3v3`), `match_bench.gd` (`--driver=inline|worker`, `--load=`, `--online_cadence=on|off`, `--holds=<n>,<ms>`, `--quality`, `--smooth`, `--physics_cap`, `--deadline`; the arm is forced per run through `force_mode_for_test`, the run fails when the pump's mode differs), `match_bench_screen.gd` (the real match screen; it drops `bench_sim` after `adopt`, takes digests only from drained notes, never enables the Profiler, keeps no overlay, cull or item counters). The scripted input track (a move every 60 ticks, a build every 600, keyed to the driver's next tick) is planned from the simulation before the pump owns it and fed through `driver.submit`; `scripted_inputs` at 0 inside the window is a bench failure ("mechanics missing: scripted inputs"). Holds print `WORKER HOLD {"hold","start","drain","take","frame"}` in wall-clock ms.
- **`worker_ab.py` and `test_worker_ab.py`:** `decide|report <paths>` from the spike's, reading every Step 1 field; the refusal (`--max-refusal-share`), worst-pace (`--min-worst-pace`) and tick-rate (`--tick-rate-tolerance`) guards are unset by default and print "unset"; the 29.7 floor is printed as a reference only; no `backlog_max`; an absurd or negative duration, a missing field, a failed or timed-out run, a one-run arm, a missing or unequal digest all give `NO VERDICT` (report prints NOTE lines). 10 test cases.
- **Red and mutations (each reverted with a replacement asserted, `git diff --exit-code` exit 0):** `test_sb5a_queue_stats_job_timings` red first on the missing `jobs` key (5 named FAILs); `test_sb5a_offline_digest_every_and_bots` red on the bare members (digest_every and bots checks, both modes). Pump: tag always `plain` -> tag check red; handoff measured from the job start -> timing check red; age fixed at 0 -> timing check red; reset without `_unread = []` -> green at first, so a check was added (a job published before the reset and drained after) and the mutation is then red. Driver: `job.bots = BOTS` -> bots check red; digest every 5 ticks regardless of the field -> digest check red. `worker_ab.py`: absurd check off -> 2 subtests red; refusal guard forced true -> red; digest comparison off -> red. Bench: the input feeder disabled -> `WORKER BENCH FAILED mechanics missing: scripted inputs`, exit 1.
- **Runs (Step 3, `t12-bench-inline.log`, `t12-bench-worker.log`; report-only):** both exit 0 with `WORKER BENCH DONE`, seven `DIGEST` lines each (ticks 6600 to 8400) and the same hashes in both arms (`DIGESTS EQUAL team (2 runs)` from `python3 tools/worker_ab.py report`). Team load, window 6600 to 8400, 1800 ticks, headless, 60 fps cap: inline `tick_rate` 30.00, `cpu_ms` p95 9.17, `pace_worst_2s_tps` 29.74, 33 scripted inputs; worker `tick_rate` 30.00, `cpu_ms` p95 11.13, `pace_worst_2s_tps` 29.64, `depth_max` 1, `room_refusals` 0 of 1800 posts, 33 scripted inputs, answer latency p50/p95 1/2 frames, `job_ms` p50/p95/max 7.60/10.94/17.70, `handoff_ms` p50/p95 1.34/1.82, `age_ms` p50/p95/max 11.05/14.76/22.77, no main waits in the window. A third run (`t12-bench-holds.log`, worker, `--holds=3,1000 --online_cadence=on`) printed three `WORKER HOLD` lines (holds 2 and 3 found the queue already empty, so `drain` equals `start`) and 60 `snapshot+digest` rows; the lower pace (`tick_rate` 28.5) is the holds (three seconds without a step), not the cadence. The floor of 29.7 ticks/s is the owner's go rule and is not judged here.
